\section{Conclusions and Future Work} \label{sec:Conclusions}

This work has proposed a physics-informed deep-learning framework for the bidirectional modeling of magnetic domains from Hamiltonian parameters, articulated around five specific objectives: a cycle-consistent inverse--forward mapping with degeneracy mitigation, the exploitation of temporal state transitions, sim-to-real domain adaptation, post-hoc interpretability, and the integration of the resulting methods into an agent-orientable tool package. Rather than resting on a single novel predictor, the contribution lies in \emph{how} these components are constructed and combined: a GPU-accelerated tensor/matrix Monte Carlo engine that generates the training corpus without altering the underlying physics; a comparison of convolutional and Vision-Transformer inverse estimators trained from scratch; a forward generator conditioned on the complete eight-parameter Hamiltonian vector; and an interpretability layer that turns the estimators into physically traceable gray-box models.

Proof-of-concept experiments on the $166{,}141$-configuration synthetic dataset already substantiate several of these components. The inverse estimators recover six of the eight Hamiltonian parameters reliably --- with Xception offering the best accuracy--size trade-off and a compact CNN matching backbones two orders of magnitude larger, showing that accuracy is bounded by the physical information in the $s_z$ projection rather than by model capacity --- while the two higher-order exchange constants remain unidentifiable, a fundamental limit of the single-axis observation rather than a modeling deficiency. Regression Activation Maps (RAMs) localize the chiral domain walls and skyrmion cores that drive the predictions and, benchmarked against SHAP, LIME, and SF-SHAP through a deletion protocol, attain a competitive faithfulness at one-to-two orders of magnitude lower computational cost. The parameter-conditioned diffusion generator reproduces the correct magnetic phase from parameters alone and matches the real spin--spin correlation function and structure factor, outperforming the conditional-VAE baseline that collapses toward a phase-averaged texture. These results establish the feasibility of the framework and motivate the objective-by-objective development plan summarized below.

\paragraph{Objective 1: Cycle-Consistent Bidirectional Mapping with Degeneracy Mitigation.}
The central strategy against degeneracy couples an inverse estimator $f_{\hat{\Theta}}:\mathbf{X}\mapsto\hat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}$ with a forward generator $g_{\Phi}:\boldsymbol{\theta}\mapsto\mathbf{X}$, both trained on the atomistic Monte Carlo corpus. The inverse pathway compares convolutional backbones (a plain CNN, ResNet, DenseNet, and Xception) against a Vision Transformer, all trained \emph{from scratch}, since the accuracy-versus-size analysis reveals no advantage from natural-image pretraining for this task. The forward pathway is a denoising diffusion model conditioned on the full Hamiltonian vector, with a conditional VAE retained only as a baseline. The novelty is that the cycle acts not as a mere consistency check but as a training mechanism: a two-phase schedule (warm-start of each block, then joint fine-tuning through the closed loop) propagates gradients across $f_{\hat{\Theta}}(g_{\Phi}(\boldsymbol{\theta}))$, discouraging the inverse estimator from collapsing degenerate configurations toward a conditional mean. Consistency is enforced by a deliberately compact objective --- reconstruction plus a cycle term --- \emph{without} the physics-based (PINN/LLG) or adversarial terms of earlier designs, and generative quality is judged by rotation- and translation-invariant physical observables rather than pixel-level error.

\paragraph{Objective 2: Exploiting State Transitions for Degeneracy Resolution.}
Because a single static $s_z$ snapshot is intrinsically ambiguous, the second objective attacks degeneracy along the temporal axis: instead of one image per state, the trajectory of state transitions induced by controlled sweeps of ambient conditions (temperature and external field) is modeled. Different microscopic interactions produce different transition dynamics, so the sequence carries discriminative information absent from any single frame; this is formalized through an information-theoretic inequality showing that the whole trajectory is at least as informative as its most informative snapshot. The intended realization couples video-diffusion generative formulations with information measures, and is the route by which the collapsed high-temperature and Curie-dominated regimes identified in the static analysis are to be revisited. This objective is under active development.

\paragraph{Objective 3: Sim-to-Real Domain Adaptation.}
To bridge the gap between synthetic simulations and experimental images, the framework favors a \emph{preprocessing}-based adaptation confined to a stage \emph{before} the trained inverse estimator, rather than an additional loss term or an auxiliary trained model --- a deliberate choice to keep the pipeline modular and to address the concern regarding methodological complexity. Several physics-aware candidates are being weighed --- physics-based forward modeling of the instrument operator, domain randomization, physics-guided image-to-image translation, and contrastive self-supervised alignment --- to be selected empirically against the experimental reference datasets (Leeds STM, Co/Pd MFM, and KIT 4D-STEM), which are already available as the target domain. Parameter estimates on experimental images will be reported as intervals through lightweight uncertainty mechanisms. This objective remains under evaluation.

\paragraph{Objective 4: Interpretability and Physical Validation.}
Interpretability is achieved \emph{post hoc} --- a choice justified by the multi-output nature of the problem, the degrees of freedom it demands, and its reuse across heterogeneous backbones and, prospectively, autonomous agents. The regression-tailored RAMs quantify, per parameter and per network layer, \emph{where} each Hamiltonian constant is inferred from, exposing a hierarchical decoupling in which early layers resolve the short-range exchange/DMI competition and deeper layers encode global thermodynamic order; a null RAM for a physically silent parameter (e.g., $\tilde{J}_2=0$ in the skyrmion phase) confirms that the network does not fabricate spurious dependencies. RAMs are complemented by latent-space analysis with UMAP --- justified over PCA and t-SNE and, for the transition series, extended with the trajectory-preserving PHATE --- and benchmarked against SHAP, LIME, and SF-SHAP, where they prove competitive in faithfulness while remaining tractable enough for per-sample, per-layer, and agent-invocable use.

\paragraph{Objective 5: Integration into an Agent-Orientable Tool Package.}
Finally, the simulation, estimation, generation, and interpretation capabilities are consolidated into a single reusable system in which each capability is a self-contained tool with a typed contract, sharing the same $\boldsymbol{\theta}$ vector, $s_z$ representation, and physical-observable definitions so that outputs chain consistently. Exposed through a standardized protocol (the Model Context Protocol), the workflow becomes discoverable and composable by external orchestrators --- including LLM-based scientific agents --- turning a collection of expert-operated scripts into an interoperable knowledge substrate; the closed loop of Objective~1 further provides an automatic, physically grounded self-check within any such chain.

\paragraph{Limitations and Future Work.}
Several directions remain open. The two higher-order exchange constants are unidentifiable from the $s_z$ projection, and the anisotropy and field terms only partially so, because their signatures reside in the discarded in-plane components $s_x,s_y$; recovering this information is one of the questions the cycle coupling and, above all, the temporal series are meant to address, and would ultimately benefit from multi-projection or full vector-field observations. The chirality degeneracy --- the sign of $\tilde{K}_{DM}$ being invisible to $s_z$ --- is explicitly left out of scope and flagged as a limitation of the observation model. Bayesian hyperparameter optimization, whose computational cost was rightly questioned during review, will be applied judiciously to the compact objective rather than to an inflated multi-term loss. The temporal (Objective~2) and domain-adaptation (Objective~3) components are still being scoped, and the agent integration (Objective~5) will be delivered as a proof of concept. Longer-term extensions include three-dimensional textures, non-equilibrium thermal dynamics beyond equilibrium Monte Carlo sampling, and active-learning loops with experimental feedback. Together, the validated components establish a physically grounded, interpretable, and reusable foundation for characterizing magnetic domains from their governing Hamiltonian parameters.
